1. <#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3. Mandatory bootstrap for UWP → WinUI 3 migration. Owns every mechanical step
4. LLMs reliably skip or get wrong. Run BEFORE any manual edit.
5. 
6. .DESCRIPTION
7. Each step below maps to the correspondingly-numbered `# ─── N.` section in
8. the code. Run `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` at the end of the SKILL.md
9. workflow to confirm residue grep, mapping integrity, deferred consistency,
10. build cleanliness, and runtime smoke.
11. 
12. Steps:
13. 1. Copy .xaml/.cs/.resw/asset/.appxmanifest from source to target, preserving folder structure
14. 2. Preserve the UWP .csproj at .uwp-source/ as a read-only reference
15. 3. Namespace mass-rewrite: Windows.UI.Xaml → Microsoft.UI.Xaml across all copied .cs/.xaml
16. 4a. Filter-prone class neutralization (RootFrameNavigationHelper → no-op stub, etc.)
17. 4b/4c. Per-file triage against unsupported-api-inventory.json + inline TODO injection
18.        (`// TODO[migrate-NNN]: see PATTERNS.md#<anchor>` — anchor-only, never an API name)
19. 5. Pre-seed MIGRATION-MAPPING.md (File / Triage / Status only — no Notes column to avoid
20.    loading the agent's context with concentrated API-name listings)
21. 6. Pre-seed MIGRATION-DEFERRED.md (generic anchor-based rationale, no API names)
22. 7. Write .bootstrap-meta.json (schema v2; includes perFileMode for BATCH vs SEQUENTIAL)
23. 8. Print a `=== BOOTSTRAP COMPLETE ===` summary block
24. 
25. Files with a sensitive-tier namespace (capture/speech/sensors/geolocation/
26. bluetooth/point-of-service/proximity) are marked SEQUENTIAL in perFileMode
27. (fix one TODO at a time, build after each). All other files are BATCH.
28. 
29. .PARAMETER Source
30. UWP project's C# source folder (contains the .csproj and Package.appxmanifest).
31. 
32. .PARAMETER Target
33. Scaffolded WinUI 3 target project root (e.g. produced by `dotnet new winui`).
34. 
35. .EXAMPLE
36. .\Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1 -Source "C:\src\UwpSample\cs" -Target "C:\out\MyWinUI3App"
37. #>
38. [CmdletBinding()]
39. param(
40.     [Parameter(Mandatory)][string]$Source,
41.     [Parameter(Mandatory)][string]$Target
42. )
43. 
44. $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
45. 
46. function Resolve-FullPath([string]$p) {
47.     return (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $p).ProviderPath
48. }
49. 
50. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Source)) { throw "Source not found: $Source" }
51. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Target)) { throw "Target not found: $Target (scaffold the WinUI 3 project first with 'dotnet new winui')" }
52. 
53. $Source = Resolve-FullPath $Source
54. $Target = Resolve-FullPath $Target
55. 
56. Write-Host "==> Initialize-UwpMigration"
57. Write-Host "    Source : $Source"
58. Write-Host "    Target : $Target"
59. 
60. # ─── 1. Copy source files (everything except .csproj) ──────────────────────────
61. $patterns = @(
62.     '.xaml', '.cs', '.resw', '.resjson',
63.     '.appxmanifest',
64.     '.png', '.jpg', '.jpeg', '.svg', '.ico', '.gif'
65. )
66. 
67. # Never copy build output / tooling folders from the UWP source. A previously-built
68. # UWP project leaves machine-generated sources under bin/ and obj/ (e.g. .NET-Native
69. # ILC *.g.cs in obj\<arch>\Release\ilc\). Those are NOT source: copying them in makes
70. # the SDK-style WinUI csproj's default `**/*.cs` glob compile thousands of duplicate
71. # types, and (for nested sub-project bin/obj) MSBuild's default obj/bin exclusion only
72. # covers the project-root obj/bin — nested ones are still compiled. Result: a build that
73. # fails or hangs with tens of thousands of CS0101/CS0227/CS0234 errors. This mirrors the
74. # $excludeDirs filter used by every other step below.
75. $srcExcludeDirs = @('bin', 'obj', '.vs', '.git', '.github', '.copilot', 'packages', 'node_modules')
76. $srcExcludePattern = '\\(' + ($srcExcludeDirs -join '|') + ')\\'
77. 
78. $copied = New-Object System.Collections.Generic.List[string]
79. 
80. Get-ChildItem -Path $Source -Recurse -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object {
81.     $rel = [System.IO.Path]::GetRelativePath($Source, $_.FullName)
82.     if (('\' + $rel) -match $srcExcludePattern) { return $false }
83.     $name = $_.Name.ToLowerInvariant()
84.     $match = $false
85.     foreach ($ext in $patterns) {
86.         if ($name.EndsWith($ext)) { $match = $true; break }
87.     }
88.     $match
89. } | ForEach-Object {
90.     $rel = [System.IO.Path]::GetRelativePath($Source, $_.FullName)
91.     $dst = Join-Path $Target $rel
92.     $dstDir = [System.IO.Path]::GetDirectoryName($dst)
93.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $dstDir)) {
94.         New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $dstDir -Force | Out-Null
95.     }
96.     Copy-Item -LiteralPath $_.FullName -Destination $dst -Force
97.     [void]$copied.Add($rel)
98. }
99. 
100. Write-Host "    Copied $($copied.Count) source files"
101. 
102. # ─── 2. Preserve UWP .csproj as read-only reference ────────────────────────────
103. $uwpCsprojs = Get-ChildItem -Path $Source -Filter '*.csproj' -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
104. $refDir = Join-Path $Target '.uwp-source'
105. if ($uwpCsprojs.Count -gt 0) {
106.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $refDir)) {
107.         New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $refDir -Force | Out-Null
108.     }
109.     foreach ($p in $uwpCsprojs) {
110.         $dst = Join-Path $refDir $p.Name
111.         Copy-Item -LiteralPath $p.FullName -Destination $dst -Force
112.         Write-Host "    Preserved $($p.Name) at .uwp-source/ (reference only — do not edit, do not include in build)"
113.     }
114. } else {
115.     Write-Warning "    No .csproj found under Source — agent has no reference for original PackageReference list"
116. }
117. 
118. # ─── 3. Namespace mass-replace: Windows.UI.Xaml → Microsoft.UI.Xaml ────────────
119. $excludeDirs = @('bin', 'obj', '.uwp-source', '.vs', '.git', '.github', '.copilot')
120. $excludePattern = '\\(' + ($excludeDirs -join '|') + ')\\'
121. $nsFiles = Get-ChildItem -Path $Target -Recurse -File -Include *.cs,*.xaml -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
122.     Where-Object { $_.FullName -notmatch $excludePattern }
123. $nsChanged = 0
124. foreach ($f in $nsFiles) {
125.     $orig = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText($f.FullName)
126.     $new = $orig -replace 'Windows\.UI\.Xaml', 'Microsoft.UI.Xaml'
127.     if ($new -ne $orig) {
128.         [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText($f.FullName, $new)
129.         $nsChanged++
130.     }
131. }
132. Write-Host "    Rewrote Windows.UI.Xaml -> Microsoft.UI.Xaml in $nsChanged of $($nsFiles.Count) .cs/.xaml files"
133. 
134. # ─── 4a. Filter-prone class neutralization ────────────────────────────────────
135. # Some SDK Samples boilerplate helpers contain UWP-specific patterns whose
136. # WinUI 3 equivalents require low-level Win32 keyboard interop. The model
137. # provider's content-safety filter routinely blocks model output containing
138. # those patterns and kills the trial. Replace those classes with no-op stubs
139. # *before* the agent ever sees them.
140. $filterProneClassNeutralizations = @(
141.     @{
142.         FilePattern  = '(^|\\)Common\\NavigationHelper\.cs$'
143.         ClassName    = 'RootFrameNavigationHelper'
144.         Reason       = 'Filter-prone: keyboard back-nav handler. WinUI 3 equivalent requires Microsoft.UI.Input keyboard-state APIs that trigger the content-safety filter. Replaced with no-op stub.'
145.         StubBody     = @'
146.         // No-op stub written by Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1.
147.         //
148.         // The original UWP implementation hooked accelerator-key activation for
149.         // ALT+Left/Right and BrowserBack/Forward, and pointer events for mouse
150.         // XButton1/XButton2. The WinUI 3 equivalent goes through low-level
151.         // keyboard-state APIs that the model provider's content-safety filter
152.         // rejects. Back-nav is not the demonstrated feature of any UWP SDK
153.         // sample, so we leave the field bound but make activation a no-op.
154.         // If you really need ALT+Left back-nav, add a single
155.         //   <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
156.         // to the AppBarButton or NavigationViewItem that triggers GoBack.
157. '@
158.     }
159. )
160. 
161. function Invoke-FilterProneNeutralization {
162.     param(
163.         [string]$FullPath,
164.         [string]$ClassName,
165.         [string]$StubBody,
166.         [string]$Reason
167.     )
168.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $FullPath)) { return $false }
169.     $text = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText($FullPath)
170.     $pattern = "(?ms)((?:public\s+|internal\s+)?class\s+$([regex]::Escape($ClassName))\b[^{]*\{)"
171.     $m = [regex]::Match($text, $pattern)
172.     if (-not $m.Success) { return $false }
173.     $bodyStart = $m.Index + $m.Length
174.     $depth = 1
175.     $i = $bodyStart
176.     while ($i -lt $text.Length -and $depth -gt 0) {
177.         $ch = $text[$i]
178.         if ($ch -eq '{') { $depth++ }
179.         elseif ($ch -eq '}') { $depth-- }
180.         $i++
181.     }
182.     if ($depth -ne 0) { return $false }
183.     $bodyEnd = $i - 1
184.     $before = $text.Substring(0, $bodyStart)
185.     $after = $text.Substring($bodyEnd)
186.     $stubCtor = "        public $ClassName(params object[] args) { /* no-op; accepts any call shape */ }`r`n"
187.     $newText = $before + "`r`n" + $StubBody + "`r`n" + $stubCtor + "    " + $after
188.     [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText($FullPath, $newText)
189.     return $true
190. }
191. 
192. $neutralizedFiles = @{}
193. foreach ($n in $filterProneClassNeutralizations) {
194.     foreach ($rel in $copied) {
195.         if ($rel -notmatch $n.FilePattern) { continue }
196.         $full = Join-Path $Target $rel
197.         $ok = Invoke-FilterProneNeutralization -FullPath $full -ClassName $n.ClassName -StubBody $n.StubBody -Reason $n.Reason
198.         if ($ok) {
199.             $key = $rel
200.             if (-not $neutralizedFiles.ContainsKey($key)) { $neutralizedFiles[$key] = @() }
201.             $neutralizedFiles[$key] += $n.ClassName
202.             Write-Host "    Neutralized class $($n.ClassName) in $rel"
203.         }
204.     }
205. }
206. 
207. # ─── 4b. Load inventory ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
208. $invPath = Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'unsupported-api-inventory.json'
209. $inv = $null
210. if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $invPath) {
211.     try { $inv = Get-Content -LiteralPath $invPath -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json } catch { Write-Warning "Failed to parse $invPath - skipping pre-triage" }
212. }
213. 
214. # Adaptable patterns with anchor (for TODO injection). Each entry: { name, pattern, anchor, tier }
215. $adaptableEntries = @()
216. if ($inv -and $inv.adaptable) {
217.     foreach ($e in $inv.adaptable) {
218.         if (-not $e.anchor) { continue }
219.         $adaptableEntries += [PSCustomObject]@{
220.             Name    = $e.name

1. <#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3. Fetch a single anchored section from MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md.
4. 
5. .DESCRIPTION
6. Use this instead of opening MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md wholesale — the full file's
7. concentrated API-name listings have historically tripped the model provider's
8. content-safety filter. This helper returns only the requested section.
9. 
10. .PARAMETER Anchor
11. Anchor ID (e.g. 'threading', 'windowing', 'dialogs', 'pickers'). The full
12. list lives in unsupported-api-inventory.json under each entry's `anchor`.
13. 
14. .PARAMETER PatternsPath
15. Optional path to MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md. Defaults to the sibling file under
16. the winui-uwp-migration skill folder.
17. 
18. .OUTPUTS
19. The section text (heading + content up to the next ## heading) on stdout.
20. Exits 0 on success, 1 if the anchor is not found.
21. 
22. .EXAMPLE
23. .\Get-MigrationPattern.ps1 -Anchor threading
24. #>
25. [CmdletBinding()]
26. param(
27.     [Parameter(Mandatory)][string]$Anchor,
28.     [string]$PatternsPath
29. )
30. 
31. $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
32. 
33. if (-not $PatternsPath) {
34.     $PatternsPath = Join-Path (Split-Path -Parent $PSScriptRoot) 'MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md'
35. }
36. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $PatternsPath)) {
37.     Write-Error "MIGRATION-PATTERNS.md not found at $PatternsPath"
38.     exit 1
39. }
40. 
41. $lines = Get-Content -LiteralPath $PatternsPath
42. $anchorPattern = "<a\s+id=`"$([regex]::Escape($Anchor))`""
43. 
44. $startIdx = -1
45. for ($i = 0; $i -lt $lines.Count; $i++) {
46.     if ($lines[$i] -match $anchorPattern) {
47.         $startIdx = $i
48.         break
49.     }
50. }
51. 
52. if ($startIdx -lt 0) {
53.     Write-Error "Anchor '#$Anchor' not found in $PatternsPath"
54.     exit 1
55. }
56. 
57. # Walk forward: skip the anchor line itself and the heading; collect until the next top-level ##.
58. $endIdx = $lines.Count - 1
59. for ($j = $startIdx + 1; $j -lt $lines.Count; $j++) {
60.     # Stop on next anchor (covers cases where two anchors precede consecutive sections)
61.     if ($lines[$j] -match '<a\s+id="' -and $j -gt $startIdx) {
62.         $endIdx = $j - 1
63.         break
64.     }
65.     # Stop on next top-level heading
66.     if ($lines[$j] -match '^##\s' -and $j -gt $startIdx + 1) {
67.         $endIdx = $j - 1
68.         break
69.     }
70. }
71. 
72. # Trim trailing blank lines for cleaner output
73. while ($endIdx -gt $startIdx -and [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($lines[$endIdx])) {
74.     $endIdx--
75. }
76. 
77. for ($k = $startIdx; $k -le $endIdx; $k++) {
78.     Write-Output $lines[$k]
79. }
80. 
--- RESULT ---

12. using System;
13. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
14. using Windows.Devices.WiFi;

16. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
17. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
18. using SDKTemplate;
19. using System.Collections.ObjectModel;
20. 
21. namespace WiFiScan
22. {
23.     public sealed partial class Scenario3_RegisterForUpdates : Page
24.     {
25.         private WiFiAdapter firstAdapter;
26. 
27.         MainPage rootPage;
28.         public ObservableCollection<WiFiNetworkDisplay> ResultCollection
29.         {
30.             get;
31.             private set;
32.         }
33. 
34.         public Scenario3_RegisterForUpdates()
35.         {
36.             this.InitializeComponent();
37.         }
38. 
39.         protected override async void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
40.         {
41.             ResultCollection = new ObservableCollection<WiFiNetworkDisplay>();
42.             rootPage = MainPage.Current;
43. 
44.             // RequestAccessAsync must have been called at least once by the app before using the API
45.             // Calling it multiple times is fine but not necessary
46.             // RequestAccessAsync must be called from the UI thread
47.             var access = await WiFiAdapter.RequestAccessAsync();
48.             if (access != WiFiAccessStatus.Allowed)
49.             {
50.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Access denied", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
51.             }
52.             else
53.             {
54.                 DataContext = this;
55. 
56.                 var result = await Windows.Devices.Enumeration.DeviceInformation.FindAllAsync(WiFiAdapter.GetDeviceSelector());
57.                 if (result.Count >= 1)
58.                 {
59.                     firstAdapter = await WiFiAdapter.FromIdAsync(result[0].Id);
60.                     RegisterButton.IsEnabled = true;
61.                 }
62.                 else
63.                 {
64.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("No WiFi Adapters detected on this machine", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
65.                 }
66.             }
67.         }
68. 
69.         private void FirstAdapter_AvailableNetworksChanged(WiFiAdapter sender, object args)
70.         {
71.             DisplayNetworkReport(sender.NetworkReport, sender);
72.         }
73. 
74.         private async void DisplayNetworkReport(WiFiNetworkReport report, WiFiAdapter adapter)
75.         {
76.             // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
77.             await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, async () =>
78.             {
79.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(string.Format("Network Report Timestamp: {0}", report.Timestamp), NotifyType.StatusMessage);
80. 
81.                 ResultCollection.Clear();
82. 
83.                 foreach (var network in report.AvailableNetworks)
84.                 {
85.                     var networkDisplay = new WiFiNetworkDisplay(network, adapter);
86.                     await networkDisplay.UpdateConnectivityLevel();
87.                     ResultCollection.Add(networkDisplay);
88.                 }
89.             });
90.         }
91. 
92.         private void Button_Click_Register(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
93.         {
94.             firstAdapter.AvailableNetworksChanged += FirstAdapter_AvailableNetworksChanged;
95.             RegisterButton.IsEnabled = false;
96.             UnregisterButton.IsEnabled = true;
97.         }
98. 
99.         private void Button_Click_Unregister(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
100.         {
101.             firstAdapter.AvailableNetworksChanged -= FirstAdapter_AvailableNetworksChanged;

184.             {

186.             }

194.             else

197.                 var credential = new PasswordCredential();
198.

200.                 // Otherwise, a System.ArgumentException will be thrown.
201.                 // The default empty password string will still be passed to the ConnectAsync method,
202.                 // which should return an "InvalidCredential" error
203.                 if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(NetworkKey.Password))
204.                 {
205.                     credential.Password = NetworkKey.Password;
206.                 }
207. 
208.                 result = await firstAdapter.ConnectAsync(selectedNetwork.AvailableNetwork, reconnectionKind, credential);
209.             }
210. 
211.             if (result.ConnectionStatus == WiFiConnectionStatus.Success)
212.             {
213.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(string.Format("Successfully connected to {0}.", selectedNetwork.Ssid), NotifyType.StatusMessage);
214.             }
215.             else
216.             {
217.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(string.Format("Could not connect to {0}. Error: {1}", selectedNetwork.Ssid, result.ConnectionStatus), NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
218.             }

<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueue

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```

WinUI 3:

```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```

Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates

- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.

### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:

- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.

Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.

### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually have

The WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).

If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:

```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```

(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)

You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:

- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you merge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder.

- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the manifest reference is the source of truth.

Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires runFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capability>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matter what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern).

5. **Carry over every UWP `<Extension>` the app's behavior depends on.** The scaffold manifest has none; the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` are activation/registration prerequisites — omitting them makes the feature fail **at runtime** (often as a silently dead control), not at build time. Copy each `<Extension>` block from the UWP source manifest (under `.uwp-source/` or the copied UWP `Package.appxmanifest`) into the WinUI 3 root manifest's `<Application>`. Common ones:

   | Extension `Category` | Feature it powers |
   |----------------------|-------------------|
   | `windows.backgroundTasks` | out-of-process `BackgroundTaskBuilder` tasks (one `<Extension EntryPoint="...">` per `TaskEntryPoint`) |
   | `windows.protocol` | custom URI-scheme activation |
   | `windows.fileTypeAssociation` | open-with / file activation |
   | `windows.appService` | background app services |
   | `windows.shareTarget` | receiving shared content |

   `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` FAILs if retained `BackgroundTaskBuilder` code has no `windows.backgroundTasks` extension; the other categories have no automated check, so verify them by diffing the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` against the migrated one.

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Threading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Search for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires ALT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalent requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code constants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Migrating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites just the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHelper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavigationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, the LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.

18. // The Blank Application template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402347&clcid=0x409
19. 
20. namespace SDKTemplate
21. {
22.     /// <summary>
23.     /// Provides application-specific behavior to supplement the default Application class.
24.     /// </summary>
25.     sealed partial class App : Application
26.     {
27.         /// <summary>
28.         /// Initializes the singleton application object.  This is the first line of authored code
29.         /// executed, and as such is the logical equivalent of main() or WinMain().
30.         /// </summary>
31.         public App()
32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34.             this.Construct();
35.         }
36. 
37.         /// <summary>
38.         /// Invoked when the application is launched normally by the end user.  Other entry points
39.         /// will be used such as when the application is launched to open a specific file.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="e">Details about the launch request and process.</param>
42.         protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs e)
43.         {
44. 
45. #if DEBUG
46.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
47.             {
48.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = false;
49.             }
50. #endif
51. 
52.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
53. 
54.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
55.             // just ensure that the window is active
56.             if (rootFrame == null)
57.             {
58.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
59.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
60.                 // Set the default language
61.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
62. 
63.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
64. 
65.                 if (e.PreviousExecutionState == ApplicationExecutionState.Terminated)
66.                 {
67.                     //TODO: Load state from previously suspended application
68.                 }
69. 
70.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
71.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
72.             }
73. 
74.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
75.             {
76.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
77.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
78.                 // parameter
79.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
80.             }
81. 
82.             // Ensure the current window is active
83.             Window.Current.Activate();
84. 
85.             // Some samples want access to the LaunchActivatedEventArgs.
86.             LaunchCompleted(e);
87.         }
88. 
89.         private Frame CreateRootFrame()
90.         {
91.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
92. 
93.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
94.             // just ensure that the window is active
95.             if (rootFrame == null)
96.             {
97.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
98.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
99. 
100.                 // Set the default language
101.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
102.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
103. 
104.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
105.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
106.             }
107. 
108.             return rootFrame;
109.         }
110. 
111.         /// <summary>
112.         /// Invoked when Navigation to a certain page fails
113.         /// </summary>
114.         /// <param name="sender">The Frame which failed navigation</param>
115.         /// <param name="e">Details about the navigation failure</param>
116.         void OnNavigationFailed(object sender, NavigationFailedEventArgs e)
117.         {
118.             throw new Exception("Failed to load Page " + e.SourcePageType.FullName);

22. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402352&clcid=0x409
23. 
24. namespace SDKTemplate
25. {
26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }

158.     {
159.         StatusMessage,
160.         ErrorMessage
161.     };

5. // THIS CODE IS PROVIDED *AS IS* WITHOUT WARRANTY OF

8. // PURPOSE, MERCHANTABILITY, OR NON-INFRINGEMENT.

11. 
12. using System;
13. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
14. using Windows.Devices.WiFi;
15. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
16. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
17. using SDKTemplate;
18. using System.Collections.ObjectModel;
19. 
20. namespace WiFiScan
21. {
22.     public sealed partial class Scenario2_Scan : Page
23.     {
24.         private WiFiAdapter firstAdapter;
25.         MainPage rootPage;
26.         public ObservableCollection<WiFiNetworkDisplay> ResultCollection
27.         {
28.             get;
29.             private set;
30.         }
31. 
32.         public Scenario2_Scan()
33.         {
34.             this.InitializeComponent();
35.         }
36. 
37.         protected override async void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
38.         {
39.             ResultCollection = new ObservableCollection<WiFiNetworkDisplay>();
40.             rootPage = MainPage.Current;
41. 
42.             // RequestAccessAsync must have been called at least once by the app before using the API
43.             // Calling it multiple times is fine but not necessary
44.             // RequestAccessAsync must be called from the UI thread
45.             var access = await WiFiAdapter.RequestAccessAsync();
46.             if (access != WiFiAccessStatus.Allowed)
47.             {
48.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Access denied", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
49.             }
50.             else
51.             {
52.                 DataContext = this;
53. 
54.                 var result = await Windows.Devices.Enumeration.DeviceInformation.FindAllAsync(WiFiAdapter.GetDeviceSelector());
55.                 if (result.Count >= 1)
56.                 {
57.                     firstAdapter = await WiFiAdapter.FromIdAsync(result[0].Id);
58. 
59.                     var button = new Button();
60.                     button.Content = string.Format("Scan");
61.                     button.Click += Button_Click;
62.                     Buttons.Children.Add(button);
63.                 }
64.                 else
65.                 {
66.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("No WiFi Adapters detected on this machine", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
67.                 }
68.             }
69.         }
70. 
71.         private async void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
72.         {
73.             await firstAdapter.ScanAsync();
74.             DisplayNetworkReport(firstAdapter.NetworkReport);
75.         }
76. 
77.         private async void DisplayNetworkReport(WiFiNetworkReport report)
78.         {
79.             rootPage.NotifyUser(string.Format("Network Report Timestamp: {0}", report.Timestamp), NotifyType.StatusMessage);
80. 
81.             ResultCollection.Clear();
82. 
83.             foreach (var network in report.AvailableNetworks)
84.             {
85.                 var networkDisplay = new WiFiNetworkDisplay(network, firstAdapter);
86.                 await networkDisplay.UpdateConnectivityLevel();
87.                 ResultCollection.Add(networkDisplay);
88.             }
89.         }
90.     }
91. }
92.

11. 
12. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
13. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
14. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
15. using SDKTemplate;
16. using System;
17. using System.Collections.Generic;
18. using Windows.Devices.WiFi;
19. using System.Collections.ObjectModel;
20. 
21. namespace WiFiScan
22. {
23.     public sealed partial class Scenario1_CachedNetworks : Page
24.     {
25.         private IReadOnlyList<WiFiAdapter> wiFiAdapters;
26.         MainPage rootPage;
27.         public ObservableCollection<WiFiNetworkDisplay> ResultCollection
28.         {
29.             get;
30.             private set;
31.         }
32. 
33.         public Scenario1_CachedNetworks()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36.         }
37. 
38.         protected override async void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
39.         {
40.             ResultCollection = new ObservableCollection<WiFiNetworkDisplay>();
41.             rootPage = MainPage.Current;
42. 
43.             // RequestAccessAsync must have been called at least once by the app before using the API
44.             // Calling it multiple times is fine but not necessary
45.             // RequestAccessAsync must be called from the UI thread
46.             var result = await WiFiAdapter.RequestAccessAsync();
47.             if (result != WiFiAccessStatus.Allowed)
48.             {
49.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Access denied", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
50.             }
51.             else
52.             {
53.                 DataContext = this;
54. 
55.                 wiFiAdapters = await WiFiAdapter.FindAllAdaptersAsync();
56.                 int index = 0;
57.                 foreach (var adapter in wiFiAdapters)
58.                 {
59.                     var button = new Button();
60.                     button.Tag = index;
61.                     button.Content = String.Format("WiFi Adapter {0}", ++index);
62.                     button.Click += Button_Click;
63.                     Buttons.Children.Add(button);
64.                 }
65.             }
66.         }
67. 
68.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
69.         {
70.             var button = (Button)sender;
71.             var index = (int)button.Tag;
72. 
73.             // Accessing the NetworkReport property from the WiFiAdapter does not trigger any scans.
74.             // The NetworkReport will have the report generated by the last scan on the system; the
75.             // scan need not be generated by the app.
76.             DisplayNetworkReport(wiFiAdapters[index].NetworkReport, wiFiAdapters[index]);
77.         }
78. 
79.         private async void DisplayNetworkReport(WiFiNetworkReport report, WiFiAdapter adapter)
80.         {
81.             rootPage.NotifyUser(string.Format("Network Report Timestamp: {0}", report.Timestamp), NotifyType.StatusMessage);
82. 
83.             ResultCollection.Clear();
84. 
85.             foreach (var network in report.AvailableNetworks)
86.             {
87.                 var networkDisplay = new WiFiNetworkDisplay(network, adapter);
88.                 await networkDisplay.UpdateConnectivityLevel();
89.                 ResultCollection.Add(networkDisplay);
90.             }
91.         }
92.     }
93. }

{"path":"C:\\Users\\xiaomgao\\AppData\\Local\\win-dev-bench\\repos\\DinahK-2SO__win-dev-skills__user--DinahK-2SO--uwp2winui3-autoevolve-260714-081556-008\\plugins\\winui-uwp-migration\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\\scripts\\Test-AppLaunch.ps1","view_range":[1,260]}
--- RESULT ---
1. <#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3. Launch an already-built WinUI 3 app and report whether it survives startup -
4. and if not, capture the REAL unhandled-exception signature from Windows Error
5. Reporting so a startup crash can be diagnosed immediately instead of guessed at.
6. 
7. .DESCRIPTION
8. A packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash on startup. When that
9. happens the only durable signal is the Windows Application event log: event 1000
10. (faulting module + native exception code) and event 1026 (.NET Runtime - the
11. managed exception TYPE, message, and stack). This script launches the built
12. layout via 'winapp run --detach --json', waits for the cold-start window, and:
13. 
14.   - if the process is alive  -> status=running  (exit 0)
15.   - if it registered then died/threw at startup -> status=crashed (exit 1),
16.     with the captured WER signature + a pointer to PATTERNS.md#startup-crashes
17.   - if it never registered (deploy/env failure: Developer Mode off, missing
18.     framework, MAX_PATH, no winapp) -> status=unavailable (exit 2) - inconclusive,
19.     NOT a code defect.
20. 
21. It NEVER throws on an operational failure; it returns a structured result and
22. (with -Json) prints it as JSON. This is the single source of truth for the
23. "did the migrated app launch" question - both Step 3 (interactive, when the app
24. first crashes) and the Step 4 validator (Section 7) call it.
25. 
26. WHY THIS MATTERS: the common failure mode is an agent that sees a startup crash
27. and hand-rolls File.WriteAllText tracing for the rest of its budget. Don't. Run
28. this once; read the captured managed exception; fix the named frame.
29. 
30. NOTE: this script is intentionally ASCII-only. The Step 4 validator invokes it
31. via 'powershell -File' (Windows PowerShell 5.1), which reads a UTF-8-no-BOM file
32. as ANSI; non-ASCII characters would corrupt parsing under 5.1.
33. 
34. .PARAMETER Target
35. The migrated WinUI 3 project root (the folder with the .csproj). The build-output
36. layout is discovered under bin/<arch>/Debug/<tfm>/win-<rid>. Use this OR -Layout.
37. 
38. .PARAMETER Layout
39. An explicit build-output layout folder (the one containing AppxManifest.xml +
40. the app .exe, or an AppX\ subfolder). Use this OR -Target.
41. 
42. .PARAMETER SettleSeconds
43. Seconds to wait after launch before the alive check. Default 10 - enough to
44. catch OnLaunched / first-Page-navigation crashes without bloating runtime.
45. 
46. .PARAMETER Json
47. Emit the structured result as JSON to stdout (for programmatic callers).
48. 
49. .OUTPUTS
50. PSCustomObject (and JSON with -Json):
51.   { ok, status, pid, aumid, layout, crash:{ code, module, managedType, message,
52.     hint, anchor } | $null, detail }
53. status in { running, crashed, unavailable }.  exit: 0 running / 1 crashed / 2 unavailable.
54. 
55. .EXAMPLE
56. .\Test-AppLaunch.ps1 -Target "C:\out\MyWinUI3App"
57. # Interactive: launch and, on crash, print the real exception + the fix pointer.
58. 
59. .EXAMPLE
60. $r = .\Test-AppLaunch.ps1 -Layout $launchFolder -Json | ConvertFrom-Json
61. if ($r.status -eq 'crashed') { "FAIL: $($r.crash.hint)" }
62. #>
63. [CmdletBinding(DefaultParameterSetName = 'Target')]
64. param(
65.     [Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName = 'Target')][string]$Target,
66.     [Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName = 'Layout')][string]$Layout,
67.     [int]$SettleSeconds = 10,
68.     [switch]$Json
69. )
70. 
71. # This script reports failures structurally; it must not abort on a non-zero
72. # external tool exit. Errors are handled explicitly per stage.
73. $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
74. 
75. function New-LaunchResult {
76.     param([bool]$Ok, [string]$Status, [string]$Detail, [hashtable]$Extra)
77.     $r = [ordered]@{
78.         ok = $Ok; status = $Status; pid = $null; aumid = $null
79.         layout = $null; crash = $null; detail = $Detail
80.     }
81.     if ($Extra) { foreach ($k in $Extra.Keys) { $r[$k] = $Extra[$k] } }
82.     [pscustomobject]$r
83. }
84. 
85. function Write-LaunchOut {
86.     param([pscustomobject]$Result)
87.     # exit code: running=0, crashed=1, unavailable=2
88.     $exit = switch ($Result.status) { 'running' { 0 } 'crashed' { 1 } default { 2 } }
89.     if ($Json) {
90.         $Result | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6
91.     } else {
92.         $tag = switch ($Result.status) { 'running' { 'OK  ' } 'crashed' { 'FAIL' } default { 'WARN' } }
93.         Write-Host ""
94.         Write-Host "==> Test-AppLaunch [$tag] status=$($Result.status)"
95.         if ($Result.layout) { Write-Host "    Layout : $($Result.layout)" }
96.         if ($Result.pid)    { Write-Host "    PID    : $($Result.pid)   AUMID: $($Result.aumid)" }
97.         if ($Result.crash) {
98.             Write-Host "    Crash  : code=$($Result.crash.code)  module=$($Result.crash.module)"
99.             if ($Result.crash.managedType) { Write-Host "    .NET   : $($Result.crash.managedType)" }
100.             if ($Result.crash.message)     { Write-Host "    Message: $($Result.crash.message)" }
101.             if ($Result.crash.hint)        { Write-Host "    Hint   : $($Result.crash.hint)" }
102.             if ($Result.crash.anchor)      { Write-Host "    Fix    : Get-MigrationPattern.ps1 -Anchor $($Result.crash.anchor)" }
103.         }
104.         if ($Result.detail) { Write-Host "    Detail : $($Result.detail)" }
105.     }
106.     exit $exit
107. }
108. 
109. # ---- Resolve the launch layout ----------------------------------------------
110. $exeNames = @()
111. if ($PSCmdlet.ParameterSetName -eq 'Target') {
112.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Target)) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Target not found: $Target") }
113.     $Target = (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Target).Path
114.     $csproj = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Target -Recurse -Filter *.csproj -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
115.         Where-Object { $_.FullName -notmatch '\\(bin|obj|\.uwp-source)\\' } | Select-Object -First 1 -ExpandProperty FullName
116.     if (-not $csproj) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "No .csproj found under $Target") }
117.     $csprojDir = Split-Path -Parent $csproj
118.     $arch = if ($env:PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE -eq 'ARM64') { 'ARM64' } else { 'x64' }
119.     $rid = $arch.ToLower()
120.     # Walk bin/<arch>/Debug and one level into the newest TFM dir, then win-<rid>.
121.     $binCandidates = @(
122.         (Join-Path $csprojDir "bin\$arch\Debug"),
123.         (Join-Path $csprojDir "bin\$rid\Debug")
124.     )
125.     foreach ($bin in $binCandidates) {
126.         if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $bin)) { continue }
127.         $tfmDir = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $bin -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
128.             Sort-Object LastWriteTime -Descending | Select-Object -First 1
129.         if (-not $tfmDir) { continue }
130.         $ridDir = Join-Path $tfmDir.FullName "win-$rid"
131.         $Layout = if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $ridDir) { $ridDir } else { $tfmDir.FullName }
132.         break
133.     }
134.     if (-not $Layout) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "No build output found under bin\$arch\Debug - build the project first.") }
135. }
136. 
137. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Layout)) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout folder not found: $Layout") }
138. $Layout = (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Layout).Path
139. 
140. # Sanity: the layout needs an exe + a manifest, else winapp run can't launch it.
141. $exes = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Layout -Filter '*.exe' -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
142. $exeNames = @($exes | ForEach-Object { [System.IO.Path]::GetFileNameWithoutExtension($_.Name) })
143. $hasMfst = (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppxManifest.xml')) -or
144.            (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppX\AppxManifest.xml'))
145. if ($exes.Count -eq 0 -or -not $hasMfst) {
146.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout is incomplete (need an .exe + AppxManifest.xml): $Layout" @{ layout = $Layout })
147. }
148. if (-not (Get-Command winapp -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
149.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "winapp CLI not on PATH - cannot launch the packaged app." @{ layout = $Layout })
150. }
151. 
152. # ---- Launch ------------------------------------------------------------------
153. $runRaw = & winapp run "$Layout" --detach --json 2>&1 | Out-String
154. $run = $null
155. try { $run = $runRaw.Trim() | ConvertFrom-Json -ErrorAction Stop } catch { }
156. $thePid = $null; $aumid = $null; $runErr = $null
157. if ($run) {
158.     foreach ($p in 'ProcessId','PID','Pid') { if ($run.$p) { $thePid = [int]$run.$p; break } }
159.     if ($run.AUMID) { $aumid = [string]$run.AUMID }
160.     if ($run.Error) { $runErr = [string]$run.Error }
161. }
162. 
163. # ---- WER capture helper ------------------------------------------------------
164. # Reads the real unhandled-exception signature for the just-crashed app:
165. #   event 1000  -> faulting module + native Exception code (e.g. 0x8001010E)
166. #   event 1026  -> .NET Runtime: managed exception TYPE + message + stack
167. # Match on the layout's exe base name(s) within a short recent window.
168. function Get-CrashSignature {
169.     param([string[]]$Names)
170.     $sig = [ordered]@{ code = $null; module = $null; managedType = $null; message = $null; hint = $null; anchor = $null }
171.     if (-not $Names -or $Names.Count -eq 0) { return $sig }
172.     $namePattern = ($Names | ForEach-Object { [regex]::Escape($_ + '.exe') }) -join '|'
173.     $events = Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 80 -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
174.         Where-Object { $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) -and $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.Message -match $namePattern }
175. 
176.     $e1000 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1000 } | Select-Object -First 1
177.     if ($e1000) {
178.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Faulting module name:\s*([^,]+)') { $sig.module = $matches[1].Trim() }
179.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Exception code:\s*(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)') { $sig.code = $matches[1].ToLower() }
180.     }
181.     $e1026 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1026 } | Select-Object -First 1
182.     if ($e1026) {
183.         # ".NET Runtime" event: "Exception Info: <Type>: <message>"
184.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception)') { $sig.managedType = $matches[1].Trim() }
185.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*[A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception[^\r\n:]*:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
186.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
187.         } elseif ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
188.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
189.         }
190.     }
191. 
192.     # Map the native exception code to a conservative hint + pattern anchor.
193.     # These name the error CLASS and point at the captured stack / the doc; they
194.     # do not assert a single fix, since the real frame is in the captured exception.
195.     switch ($sig.code) {
196.         '0x80004003' {
197.             $sig.hint   = "E_POINTER - most often the static-window init-order race: a Page read App.MainWindow (or another static window ref) before OnLaunched assigned it. Keep MainWindow's ctor inert; navigate after Activate."
198.             $sig.anchor = 'windowing'
199.         }
200.         '0x8001010e' {
201.             $sig.hint   = "RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD - a thread/apartment-affined object was accessed during startup (commonly a view/CoreWindow-affined UWP API touched from a static initializer or off the UI thread). Construct/access it on the UI thread after Activate; if the API is unsupported on WinUI 3 desktop, defer it."
202.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
203.         }
204.         '0xe0434352' {
205.             $sig.hint   = "Managed .NET exception - see the .NET exception type captured above (event 1026). Resolve that type (e.g. TypeLoad / FileNotFound usually means a missing or incompatible package reference)."
206.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
207.         }
208.         '0xc000027b' {
209.             $sig.hint   = "Native stowed exception - frequently a legacy projection/activation incompatibility. If a UWP API/contract used at startup is unsupported on this OS, defer it per MIGRATION-DEFERRED.md."
210.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
211.         }
212.         default {
213.             $sig.hint   = "Startup crash - read the captured exception (event 1026) to find the throwing frame."
214.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
215.         }
216.     }
217.     return $sig
218. }
219. 
220. # ---- Classify ----------------------------------------------------------------
221. if ($thePid) {
222.     Start-Sleep -Seconds $SettleSeconds
223.     $alive = $null -ne (Get-Process -Id $thePid -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
224.     if ($alive) {
225.         # Best-effort cleanup so we don't leave the dev-registered app running.
226.         try { Stop-Process -Id $thePid -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue } catch {}
227.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $true 'running' "App stayed alive ${SettleSeconds}s after launch (pid $thePid)." @{ layout = $Layout; pid = $thePid; aumid = $aumid })
228.     } else {
229.         $crash = Get-CrashSignature -Names $exeNames
230.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'crashed' "App launched (pid $thePid) but exited within ${SettleSeconds}s - startup crash." @{ layout = $Layout; aumid = $aumid; crash = [pscustomobject]$crash })
231.     }
232. } else {
233.     # No PID. Distinguish a startup crash from a deploy/env failure by whether the
234.     # app actually got REGISTERED/ACTIVATED (winapp emits an AUMID once it does).
235.     if ($aumid) {
236.         # Registered + activated, then threw before it could report a PID => startup crash (DEFECT).
237.         $crash = Get-CrashSignature -Names $exeNames
238.         $detail = "App registered (AUMID present) but crashed during activation/startup"
239.         if ($runErr) { $detail = $detail + " - winapp reported '" + $runErr + "'" }
240.         $detail = $detail + "."
241.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'crashed' $detail @{ layout = $Layout; aumid = $aumid; crash = [pscustomobject]$crash })
242.     } else {
243.         # Never registered => deployment/environment problem, not a code defect. Inconclusive.
244.         $detail = "winapp run did not register the app (no AUMID, no PID)"
245.         if ($runErr) { $detail = $detail + " - '" + $runErr + "'" }
246.         $detail = $detail + ". Usually Developer Mode off, a missing framework dependency, cert issue, or MAX_PATH - not a migration defect."
247.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' $detail @{ layout = $Layout })
248.     }
249. }
250.

<a id="startup-crashes"></a>
## Diagnosing Startup Crashes

A packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash the instant it launches — `winapp run` returns no usable window and the process is gone. The build log and the analyzer cannot see this; only a real launch does. The **wrong** way to chase it is to sprinkle `File.WriteAllText` traces through `Program.cs` / `App.xaml.cs` and re-run repeatedly — that burns time and usually misses the real frame (the crash often happens in native activation, before your managed `Main` even runs).

**Capture the real exception first.** Either run the launch-and-diagnose helper:

```powershell
& "<skill-root>/scripts/Test-AppLaunch.ps1" -Target "<winui3-project-root>"
```

…or read the Windows **Application** event log directly for the two events the crash writes:

```powershell
Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 40 |
  Where-Object { $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) } |
  ForEach-Object { "`n=== Event $($_.Id) @ $($_.TimeCreated) ==="; $_.Message }
```

- **Event 1000** — faulting module + native **Exception code** (e.g. `0x8001010E`).
- **Event 1026** — *.NET Runtime*: the managed **exception type, message, and stack**. This is the one that names the actual throwing frame. Read it before changing any code.

**Known native codes and what they usually mean** (the code names the error *class*; the real fix is whatever the captured stack points at):

| Exception code | Meaning | Usual migration cause → where to look |
|---|---|---|
| `0x80004003` | `E_POINTER` | Static-window **init-order race** — a `Page` read `App.MainWindow` (or another static window reference) before `OnLaunched` assigned it. Keep `MainWindow`'s constructor inert and navigate after `Activate`. See [Initialization order](#windowing). |
| `0x8001010E` | `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` | A **thread/apartment-affined object** was accessed during startup — commonly a view- or `CoreWindow`-affined UWP API touched from a `static` initializer, a type constructor, or off the UI thread. Construct/access it on the UI thread *after* `Activate`. If the API has no WinUI 3 desktop equivalent, defer it. |
| `0xE0434352` | Managed CLR exception | Read the **.NET exception type** in event 1026. `TypeLoadException` / `FileNotFoundException` almost always means a missing or version-incompatible package reference, not your code. |
| `0xC000027B` | Native stowed exception | Often a legacy projection/activation incompatibility for an API used at startup. If the API/contract is unsupported on the current OS, defer it. |

> Do **not** assume the entry point is the problem. A custom `Program.Main` for WinUI 3 **correctly** carries `[STAThread]` + `ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers()` + the `DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext` setup — this matches the SDK's auto-generated `Main`. `[STAThread]` is **required**, not a bug. If you have a hand-written entry point and don't need single-instancing/redirection, the simplest path is to delete it and let the SDK generate `Main`.

The Step 4 validator runs this same check (`Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7) and **fails** when the app registers but dies at startup, surfacing the captured signature in `.validator-diagnostics.txt`.
<shellId: 12 completed with exit code 0>
